Milstein-type schemes for McKean–Vlasov SDEs driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure (with super-linear coefficients)
Sani Biswas * {}^{\mbox{\textasteriskcentered}}, Chaman Kumar † {}^{\mbox{\textdagger}}, Christoph Reisinger ‡ {}^{\mbox{\textdaggerdbl}}, Verena Schwarz § {}^{\mbox{\textsection}}

Abstract
. In this work, we present a general Milstein-type scheme for McKean–Vlasov stochastic differential equations (SDEs) driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure and the associated system of interacting particles where drift, diffusion and jump coefficients may grow super-linearly in the state variable and linearly in the measure component. The strong rate of ℒ 2 \mathcal{L}^{2} -convergence of the proposed scheme is shown to be arbitrarily close to one under appropriate regularity assumptions on the coefficients. For the derivation of the Milstein scheme and to show its strong rate of convergence, we provide an Itô formula for the interacting particle system connected with the McKean–Vlasov SDE driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure. Moreover, we use the notion of Lions derivative to examine our results. The two-fold challenges arising due to the presence of the empirical measure and super-linearity of the jump coefficient are resolved by identifying and exploiting an appropriate coercivity-type condition.



Keywords.
McKean–Vlasov equation, Lévy noise, super-linear coefficient, interacting particle system, Lions derivatives.

AMS Subject Classifications.
65C05, 65C30, 65C35, 60H35.

1.
Introduction

Consider a probability space
(\Omega,\mathscr{F},\{\mathscr{F}_{t}\}_{t\geq 0},{P})
satisfying the usual conditions of completeness and right continuity, which supports a standard Brownian motion
w:=\{w_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\}_{t\geq 0}
and an independent Poisson random measure
n_{p}(dt,dz)
having compensator
\nu(dz)dt
, where
\nu(Z)<\infty
is the Lévy measure defined on a
\sigma-
finite measurable space
(Z,\mathscr{Z})
. Further, define the compensated Poisson random measure by
\tilde{n}_{p}(dt,dz):=n_{p}(dt,dz)-\nu(dz)\,dt
.
Also, suppose that
\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is the space of square integrable probability measures on the measurable space
(\mathbb{R}^{d},\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, equipped with the
\mathcal{L}^{2}
-Wasserstein metric

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2}):=\inf_{\pi\in\Pi(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})}\Big[\int_{\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}}|x-y|^{2}\pi(dx,dy)\Big]^{1/2}
where
\Pi(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})
is the set of all couplings of
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
Clearly,
\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
is a complete metric space under the metric
\mathcal{W}_{2}
.
Let
b:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\sigma:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
be
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
-measurable functions and let
\gamma:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times Z\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
be a
\mathscr{B}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\otimes\mathscr{B}(\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))\otimes\mathscr{Z}
-measurable function.
For a fixed constant
T>0
, consider the McKean–Vlasov stochastic differential equation (SDE) driven by Brownian motion and compensated Poisson random measure as given below,

(1)

\displaystyle x_{t}=x_{0}+

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}b(x_{s},\mu_{s}^{x})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(x_{s},\mu_{s}^{x})dw_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\gamma(x_{s},\mu_{s}^{x},z)\tilde{n}_{p}(ds,dz)

almost surely for any
t\in[0,T]
, where
\mu^{x}_{t}
denotes the law of
x_{t}
.
The defining characteristic of McKean–Vlasov SDEs is the presence of the law
\mu^{x}_{t}
in the coefficients, which necessitates further investigation beyond that for classical SDEs.
McKean–Vlasov equations such as (
1
) and its special cases are widely used in different fields, including finance, biological sciences, neuroscience, statistical physics and machine learning, see
[
2
,
7
,
8
,
11
,
12
,
13
,
18
,
20
,
22
,
23
,
24
,
27
,
30
,
31
,
39
,
47
]
and the references therein.
Even more so than classical SDEs, McKean–Vlasov SDEs too are often difficult to solve explicitly, as the law
\mu^{x}_{t}
is typically unknown, and this has led to keen interest by researchers in constructing numerical solutions of such equations.
Broadly speaking, most numerical schemes for McKean–Vlasov SDEs can be broken into two steps.
First, the law
\mu^{x}_{t}
is approximated by the empirical law and then a time-discretization is performed.
Indeed, consider
N

i.i.d
copies
\{x_{0}^{1},\ldots,x_{0}^{N}\}
of
x_{0}
,
\{w^{1},\ldots,w^{N}\}
of
w
,
\{\tilde{n}_{p}^{1},\ldots,\tilde{n}_{p}^{N}\}
of
\tilde{n}_{p}
and define the
non-interacting particle system
as

(2)

\displaystyle x^{i}_{t}=

\displaystyle x^{i}_{0}+\int_{0}^{t}b(x^{i}_{s},\mu_{s}^{x})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(x^{i}_{s},\mu_{s}^{x})dw^{i}_{s}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\gamma(x^{i}_{s},\mu_{s}^{x},z)\tilde{n}_{p}^{i}(ds,dz)

almost surely for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
where
\mu_{t}^{x^{i}}=\mu_{t}^{x}
.
On estimating the law
\mu_{t}^{x}
by the empirical law

\mu_{t}^{x,N}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\delta_{x_{t}^{i,N}},

one obtains the following
interacting particle system

(3)

\displaystyle x_{t}^{i,N}

\displaystyle=x_{0}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}b(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N})ds+\int_{0}^{t}\sigma(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N})dw_{s}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\gamma(x^{i,N}_{s},\mu_{s}^{x,N},z)\tilde{n}_{p}^{i}(ds,dz)

almost surely for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Equation (
2
) can be considered to be the limiting case of (
3
) when
N\to\infty
and this phenomenon is known as the
propagation of chaos
(PoC).
Then, one can discretize (
3
) to obtain fully discretized numerical schemes.
The aim of this article is to construct a Milstein-type numerical scheme and investigate its rate of strong convergence.

Existing Results

The strong well-posedness and PoC of McKean–Vlasov equations
have been widely discussed in the literature.
When
\gamma\equiv 0
and the coefficients
b
and
\sigma
are Lipschitz continuous in both state and measure variables, the well-posedness and PoC are known from
[
11
]
.
Further,
[
12
]
provides well-posedness and PoC for McKean–Vlasov SDE with common noise under Lipschitz continuity of all coefficients in both the variables.
An extension of the results of
[
11
]
for super-linearly growing drift coefficient in the state variable is done in
[
44
]
, while
[
34
]
extends the results of both
[
11
,
44
]
by including super-linear growth in the state variable of all the coefficients. Recently,
[
15
]
proved well-posedness and PoC for McKean–Vlasov SDEs when the drift coefficient exhibits a convolution-type non-linearity in the measure, which is further extended to include super-linear diffusion coefficients of a similar type, with additional results on ergodicity, in
[
16
]
.
Finally,
[
41
]
extends the results of
[
34
]
in the absence of common noise to the McKean–Vlasov SDE driven by Lévy noise by identifying a suitable monotonicity- and coercivity-type conditions on the coefficients, allowing all the coefficients (including the jump coefficient) to grow super-linearly.
We refer the interested readers to the relevant references mentioned on the articles listed above for a more comprehensive discussion of the subject.

The time discretization of the interacting particle system associated with the McKean–Vlasov SDE was initiated in
[
21
]
, where an explicit (drift-tamed) Euler-type scheme is proposed and its strong order is shown to be
1/2
.
There, the drift coefficient is required to satisfy a one-sided Lipschitz condition and the diffusion coefficient is assumed to be Lipschitz continuous in the state variable, while with respect to the measure component, both of them are taken to be Lipschitz continuous.
Furthermore, an explicit first-order Milstein-type scheme for the interacting particle system related to McKean–Vlasov SDEs (with super-linear drift coefficient in the state variable) was first developed in
[
4
,
33
]
(simultaneously but independently) with the help of the notion of Lions derivative.
Moreover, half- and first-order (tamed) schemes for such interacting particle systems with common noise are investigated in
[
34
]
under appropriate coercivity and monotonicity assumptions on the coefficients with respect to the state variable.
Tamed schemes for McKean–Vlasov SDEs with super-linearly growing coefficients as mentioned above and hereafter are inspired by the corresponding seminal works
[
28
,
29
]
on the tamed order-half schemes for classical SDEs.
We also refer the interested reader to
[
43
]
for an adaptive Euler scheme for interacting particle systems with super-linearly growing coefficients in the state variable and its application to a mean-field FitzHugh–Nagumo model from neuroscience,
[
38
]
for Euler schemes for interacting particle systems under local Lipschitz conditions in the state component,
[
3
]
for McKean–Vlasov SDE with irregular coefficients,
[
1
]
for numerical approximation of McKean–Vlasov SDEs using stochastic gradient descent, which avoids the use of interacting particle systems,
[
14
]
for a flexible split‐step scheme for McKean–Vlasov SDEs and its half order of convergence,
[
15
,
16
]
for the numerical approximation of fully super-linear convolution-type McKean–Vlasov SDEs and the associated interacting particle systems,
[
5
]
for Milstein schemes and antithetic multi-level Monte Carlo sampling for McKean–Vlasov SDEs with delay and
[
6
]
for a randomized scheme for McKean–Vlasov SDE with common noise without assuming any derivative conditions on the drift coefficient, along with the references contained therein.

Recently, motivated by the techniques developed in
[
19
,
35
]
for classical SDEs with super-linear drift coefficient, an explicit half-order Euler-type scheme for the interacting particle system coupled with McKean–Vlasov SDEs driven by Lévy noise is proposed and analysed in
[
41
]
,
which also allows super-linear growth of the jump coefficient in the state variable under suitable coercivity and monotonicity conditions.
To the best of our knowledge, there is no literature on higher order schemes such as Milstein-type schemes for McKean–Vlasov SDEs driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure, even for Lipschitz coefficients, which is the main objective of this article.

Challenges and Novelty

Even though the interacting particle system in (
3
) can be regarded as an
N\times d
-dimensional SDE driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure, known results on time-discretization schemes for SDEs driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure or Lévy noise – see, for example,
[
19
,
28
,
29
,
36
]
and the references therein – cannot be used because of the dependence of the constants appearing in their proofs on the dimension
N\times d
, which explodes in the limiting case
N\to\infty
.
This is a general challenge for all time-discrete schemes for interacting particle systems and thus is present for our Milstein-type scheme.
However, this difficulty becomes more pronounced in our case mainly due to the presence of additional terms in the numerical approximation scheme arising from the empirical law, including measure derivatives and differences of coefficients with slightly different measure components.
This is handled by a careful manipulation and a type of Taylor expansion given in Lemma
2.3
.
Moreover, we allow the jump coefficient to grow super-linearly in the state variable. This has not been treated in the literature for higher-order schemes, even in the case of standard SDEs driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure. The approximation of SDEs driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure with a super-linear jump coefficient using an Euler–Maruyama scheme is studied in
[
17
]
.
The super-linearity of the jump coefficient plus the interaction of the particles through the empirical measure brings additional complexities in comparison with existing results
[
32
,
33
,
36
]
on super-linear drift and diffusion coefficients. On the one hand, it requires additional taming of all the terms in the Milstein-type scheme that include the jump coefficient, which makes additional assumptions necessary. On the other hand, we have to identify appropriate conditions for our coefficients that allow for a super-linear jump coefficient. These conditions have not appeared in literature before, and thus, a different approach is required to investigate our Milstein-type scheme in this setting. The main difficulty in the proofs arises from the complexity of the scheme due to the versatile interaction of the coefficients in the approximation scheme. These make very careful estimates necessary and require us at certain points to use more advanced remainders in the Taylor like expansion of our coefficients than in the previous literature, see Lemma
2.3
.

Our Contribution

We propose a Milstein-type scheme for the interacting particle system in (
3
) associated with the McKean–Vlasov SDE (
1
).
The drift, diffusion and jump coefficients are assumed to satisfy a polynomial Lipschitz condition in the state variable and Lipschitz condition in the measure variable,
i.e.
in the
\mathcal{W}_{2}
-Wasserstein metric.
New coercivity-type and growth conditions are identified to allow for a super-linear growth of the jump coefficient in the state variable.
The first and second order derivatives of the coefficients with respect to the state component are assumed to be bounded, while we use the notion of Lions derivative (see
[
10
]
) to differentiate the coefficients with respect to the measure component.
Further regularity on the first order derivatives of the coefficients with respect to the state and measure component are assumed.
In order to develop a Milstein-type scheme for the interacting particle system in (
3
) and to investigate the rate of its strong convergence, we derive a version of Itô’s formula for the interacting particle system by the empirical projection method, which is different from
[
25
,
37
]
.
To be able to treat the cases of super-linear coefficients in the state component, which make a taming of the Milstein-type scheme necessary, and Lipschitz continuous coefficients at once, we define a general approximation scheme in terms of a sequence.
In this setting we prove that the strong
\mathcal{L}^{2}
-convergence of the Milstein-type scheme (
4
) is arbitrarily close to one.
The general conditions allow the proposed class of taming schemes to be applied to a large variety of McKean–Vlasov equations, with the tamed coefficients adapted to the problem at hand. We defer concrete examples and their implementation to a subsequent paper.

Notations

The euclidean norm on
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and the matrix norm on
\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
are both expressed using the same notation,
|\cdot|
.
The Borel
\sigma
-algebra on a topological space
\mathcal{T}
is denoted by the symbol
\mathscr{B}(\mathcal{T})
.
The symbol
A^{u}
is used to signify the
u
-th element of a vector,
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
for any
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
.
We use
A^{v}
and
A^{(u)}
to denote the
v
-th column and the
u
-th row of a matrix
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
, respectively for any
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
and
v\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.
Furthermore, we denote the
(u,v)
-th entry of a matrix,
A\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}
by
A^{uv}
, for any
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
and
v\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
. The notation
A^{*}
and
tr(A)
are used to denote transpose and trace of a matrix
A
.
The Dirac measure with a center at
a\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
is denoted as
\delta_{a}
, and the indicator function of a set
A
is denoted as
1_{A}
.
The gradient and Hessian matrix of
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with respect to the state variable are represented by
\partial_{x}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\partial_{x}^{2}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, respectively.
Also,
\partial_{\mu}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\partial_{\mu}^{2}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
are used to denote the first- and second- order Lions derivatives (derivative with respect to the measure component in the sense of
[
10
]
) of the function
f(x,\mu)
, respectively.
Furthermore,
\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
and
\partial_{y}\partial_{\mu}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
are used to denote the gradient of
\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)
with respect to
x
and
y
, respectively.
The notation
\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
refers to the set of continuous functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}
with continuous second-order partial derivatives everywhere.

Also, we use
\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
to denote the class of continuous functions
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
having continuous derivatives
\partial_{x}f(x,\mu)
,
\partial_{x}^{2}f(x,\mu)
,
\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)
,
\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)
,
\partial_{y}\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)
and
\partial_{\mu}^{2}f(x,\mu,y,y^{\prime})
, for any
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
x,y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
For
f\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))
, we define the following operators

\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{b}f(x,\mu):=\partial_{x}f(x,\mu)b(x,\mu),

\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{b}f(x,\mu,y):=\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)b(y,\mu),

\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}f(x,\mu):=\partial_{x}f(x,\mu)\sigma^{\ell_{1}}(x,\mu),

\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}}f(x,\mu,y):=\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)\sigma^{\ell_{1}}(y,\mu),

\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{\gamma(z)}f(x,\mu):=\partial_{x}f(x,\mu)\gamma(x,\mu,z),

\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{\gamma(z)}f(x,\mu,y):=\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)\gamma(y,\mu,z),

\mathfrak{D}_{xx}^{\sigma}f(x,\mu):=tr[\partial_{x}^{2}f(x,\mu)\sigma(x,\mu)\sigma^{*}(x,\mu)],

\mathfrak{D}_{x\mu}^{\sigma}f(x,\mu):=tr[\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,x)\sigma(x,\mu)\sigma^{*}(x,\mu)],

\mathfrak{D}_{y\mu}^{\sigma}f(x,\mu,y)\hskip-0.28436pt:=tr[\partial_{y}\partial_{\mu}f(x,\mu,y)\sigma(y,\mu)\sigma^{*}(y,\mu)],

\mathfrak{D}_{\mu\mu}^{\sigma}f(x,\mu,y):=tr[\partial_{\mu}^{2}f(x,\mu,y,y)\sigma(y,\mu)\sigma^{*}(y,\mu)]

for any
\ell_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
,
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
z\in Z
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
.
The above operators are used in the Itô’s formula (
i.e.
, Lemma
2.1
) with
f(\cdot,\cdot)=F(\cdot,\cdot)
and subsequently in the derivation and investigation of the rate of convergence of the Milstein-type scheme with
f(\cdot,\cdot)=b^{u}(\cdot,\cdot),\sigma^{u\ell}(\cdot,\cdot),\gamma^{u}(\cdot,\cdot,\bar{z})
for every
\bar{z}\in Z
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.
If
f
is additionally a function of
Z
(
i.e.
, when
f(\cdot,\cdot)=\gamma^{u}(\cdot,\cdot,\bar{z})
), then there is an additional dependence on
\bar{z}\in Z
of these operators and this should be clear from the context.
We write
EX
for expectation of a random variable
X
and
E_{t}X
for the conditional expectation of
X
given
\mathscr{F}_{t}
.
The minimum of two real numbers
a
and
b
is symbolised by
a\wedge b
and their maximum by
a\vee b
.
We use the notation
K
to denote the positive generic constant which may vary from place to place but never depends on
N
and the discretization step-size.

2.
Main result and Relevant pre-requisites

In this section, we first recall the well-posedness and moment stability of the McKean–Vlasov SDE (
1
) driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure and the associated interacting particle system (
3
), and a
propagation of chaos
result.
Moreover, this article’s main result on the rate of strong convergence of the proposed Milstein-type scheme (
4
) below for the interacting particle system (
3
)
is given in Theorem
2.1
, with its proof in Section
4
.

2.1.
Well-posedness and Propagation of Chaos

For well-posedness of the McKean–Vlasov SDE (
1
) and the connected interacting particle system (
3
), let us make the following assumptions.

First, fix
\bar{p}>4
.

Assumption 2.1
.

E|x_{0}|^{\bar{p}}<\infty
.

Assumption 2.2
.

There exist constants
C>0
and
\alpha>1
such that for any
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in~\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle 2(x-x^{\prime})\big(b(x,\mu)-b(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime}))+\alpha|\sigma(x,\mu)-\sigma(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle+\alpha\int_{Z}|\gamma(x,\mu,z)-\gamma(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq C\{|x-x^{\prime}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\}.

Assumption 2.3
.

There is a constant
C>0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle 2xb(x,\mu)+|\sigma(x,\mu)|^{2}+\int_{Z}|\gamma(x,\mu,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq C\{1+|x|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\}.

Assumption 2.4
.

For every
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, the map
\mathbb{R}^{d}\ni x\mapsto b(x,\mu)
is continuous.

Assumption 2.5
.

There is a constant
C>0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle 2|x|^{{\bar{p}}-2}xb(x,\mu)+(\bar{p}-1)|x|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big|\sigma(x,\mu)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+2(\bar{p}-1)\int_{Z}\big|\gamma(x,\mu,z)\big|^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\big|x+\theta\gamma(x,\mu,z)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}d\theta\nu(dz)\leq C\big\{1+|x|^{\bar{p}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\bar{p}}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big\}.

The following proposition is proved in
[
41
, Theorem 2.1]
.

Proposition 2.1
.

If Assumptions
2.1
to
2.4
are satisfied, then there exists a unique solution of the McKean–Vlasov SDE (
1
) as well as of its associated interacting particle system (
3
).
Further, when Assumption
2.5
also holds, then there exists a constant
K>0
such that, for all
N\in\mathbb{N}

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}E|x_{t}|^{\bar{p}}\leq K\mbox{ and }\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}E|x_{t}^{i,N}|^{\bar{p}}\leq K.

The asymptotic behaviour of the interacting particles system (
3
) is shown in the following proposition, which is known in the literature as
propagation of chaos
and its proof can be found in
[
41
]
.

Proposition 2.2
.

If Assumptions
2.1
to
2.4
are satisfied, then the rate of convergence of the interacting particle system (
3
) to the non-interacting particle system (
2
) is given by

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\,\,\sup_{t\in[0,T]}E|x_{t}^{i}-x_{t}^{i,N}|^{2}\leq K\begin{cases}N^{-1/2},&\mbox{ if }d<4,\\
N^{-1/2}\ \log_{2}N,&\mbox{ if }d=4,\\
N^{-2/d}&\mbox{ if }d>4,\end{cases}

where
K>0
is a constant independent of
N
.

2.2.
Milstein-type Scheme and Main Result

For the purpose of defining our scheme, we introduce the following sequences.
Given
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, consider

\big\{(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,y)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\gamma(z)}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\gamma(z)}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,y)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\gamma}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,\bar{z})\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,\bar{z})\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,y,\bar{z})\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\gamma(z)}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x,\mu,\bar{z}\big)\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

\big\{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\gamma(z)}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,y,\bar{z})\big\}_{n\in\mathbb{N}}

for any
\ell,\ell_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
.
We drop the superscript ‘
u
’ from the above expressions for the (
d\times 1
) vector representation of the above expressions.
The interval
[0,T]
is partitioned into
n\in\mathbb{N}
sub-intervals
0=t_{0}^{n}<t_{1}^{n}<\cdots<t_{n-1}^{n}<t_{n}^{n}=T
and define for
n\in\mathbb{N}
,
k\in\{1,\ldots,n\}
and
t\in[t_{k-1},t_{k})
,
\kappa_{n}(t):=t_{k-1}
.
Now, we propose the following Milstein-type scheme for the interacting particle system (
3
)

\displaystyle x_{t}^{i,N,n}=x_{0}^{i}+\int_{0}^{t}(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)ds

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\Big\}dw_{s}^{i}

(4)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\Big\{(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\Big\}\tilde{n}_{p}^{i}(ds,d\bar{z})

almost surely for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
where
(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}
for
\mathfrak{q}=1,2
are
d\times m
matrices whose
u\ell
-th elements are

\displaystyle(\widehat{\sigma}_{1}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big):=\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{m}\int_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{s}{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n}\big)}dw_{r}^{i\ell_{1}}

(5)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{s}{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}\big)}dw_{r}^{k\ell_{1}},

\displaystyle(\widehat{\sigma}_{2}^{u\ell}

\displaystyle)_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{s}\int_{Z}\big\{(\widehat{\sigma}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}\big(x_{{\kappa_{n}(s)}}^{i,N,n}+1_{\{k=i\}}(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},z\big),\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(k,z),N,n}\big)

(6)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(\widehat{\sigma}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big\}n_{p}^{k}(dr,dz)

respectively, and
(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}
for
\mathfrak{q}=1,2
are
d\times 1
vectors whose
u
-th components are

\displaystyle(\widehat{\gamma}_{1}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big):=\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{m}\int_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{s}{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},\bar{z}\big)}dw_{r}^{i\ell_{1}}

(7)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{s}{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n},\bar{z}\big)}dw_{r}^{k\ell_{1}},

\displaystyle(\widehat{\gamma}_{2}^{u}

\displaystyle)_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)

\displaystyle:=\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{s}\int_{Z}\big\{(\widehat{\gamma}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}\big(x_{{\kappa_{n}(s)}}^{i,N,n}+1_{\{k=i\}}(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},z\big),\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(k,z),N,n},\bar{z}\big)

(8)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-(\widehat{\gamma}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big\}n_{p}^{k}(dr,dz)

respectively, for any
s\in[0,T]
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
\bar{z}\in Z
where

(9)

\displaystyle\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{j,N,n}},\quad\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(k,z),N,n}:=\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{j,N,n}+1_{\{j=k\}}(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{j,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},z\big)}.

Remark 2.1
.

We do not restrict ourselves to any specific form of taming for the Milstein-type scheme in (
4
). Instead, the scheme is defined through sequences satisfying certain conditions, e.g., Assumptions
2.6
to
2.9
and
2.15
to
2.18
mentioned below.
Specifically, this includes the case when the coefficients are Lipschitz continuous, i.e., of linear growth in both state and measure variables.
It is worth mentioning that the tamed Milstein scheme for SDEs with Lévy noise developed in
[
36
,
33
]
can be considered as a special case of the Milstein-type scheme considered in this article when the law
\mu_{t}
is known and the jump coefficient is linear.
Similarly, our scheme in (
4
) becomes the classical Milstein scheme for SDEs driven by Lévy noise, see
[
42
]
, when all the coefficients are of linear growth and the law
\mu_{t}
is given.

Now, we present the convergence rate of the scheme (
4
) for (
3
) in Theorem
2.1
below under the following set of assumptions.
Fix
\eta>0
.

Assumption 2.6
.

For some
\bar{p}>4
, there exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle 2|x|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)+(\bar{p}-1)|x|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)\big|^{2}+2(\bar{p}-1)\int_{Z}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,z)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\big|x+\theta(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,z)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}d\theta\nu(dz)\leq C\big\{1+|x|^{\bar{p}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\bar{p}}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big\}

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Assumption 2.7
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\Big\{n^{\frac{1}{3}}\big(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big),|{b}(x,\mu)|\Big\},

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\Big\{n^{\frac{1}{6}}\big(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big),|{\sigma}(x,\mu)|\Big\},

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\Big\{n^{\frac{1}{6}}\big(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big),\big|{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu)\big|\Big\},

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,y)|

\displaystyle\leq C\min\Big\{n^{\frac{1}{6}}\big(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big),\big|{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu,y)\big|\Big\},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq C\min\Big\{n^{\frac{1}{4}}\big(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big)^{\bar{p}},\int_{Z}|{\gamma}(x,\mu,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)\Big\},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}|(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq C\min\Big\{n^{\frac{\bar{p}}{4}}\big(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big)^{\bar{p}},\int_{Z}|{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)\Big\},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}|(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,y,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq C\min\Big\{n^{\frac{\bar{p}}{4}}\big(1+|x|+\mathcal{W}(\mu,\delta_{0})\big)^{\bar{p}},\int_{Z}|{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,y,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)\Big\}

for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\ell,\ell_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Assumption 2.8
.

There exist a constant
C>0
and
\bar{C}:Z\mapsto(0,\infty)
with
\displaystyle\int_{Z}|\bar{C}_{z}|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)<~\infty
such that for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
z\in Z

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mu}b^{u}(x,\mu,y)|+|\partial_{\mu}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu,y)|\leq\bar{C}\mbox{ and }|\partial_{\mu}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,y,z)|\leq\bar{C}_{z}.

Assumption 2.9
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\sigma}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu)-(\widehat{\sigma}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\bar{\mu})|

\displaystyle\leq C|\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu)-\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\bar{\mu})|,

\displaystyle|(\widehat{\gamma}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\mu,z)-(\widehat{\gamma}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}(x,\bar{\mu},z)|

\displaystyle\leq C|\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,z)-\gamma^{u}(x,\bar{\mu},z)|,\,\mbox{ for $z\in Z$}

for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu,\bar{\mu}\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
.

Assumption 2.10
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle|b(x,\mu)-b(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq C\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\eta}|x-x^{\prime}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\}.

Assumption 2.11
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle\int_{Z}|\gamma(x,\mu,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)\leq C\{1+|x|^{\frac{\eta}{2}+\bar{p}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{\bar{p}}(\mu,\delta_{0})\}.

Assumption 2.12
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle\int_{Z}|\partial_{x}\gamma(x,\mu,z)|^{\bar{p}}\nu(dz)\leq C\{1+|x|^{\frac{\eta}{2}}\}.

Assumption 2.13
.

There exist a constant
C>0
and
\bar{C}:Z\mapsto(0,\infty)
with
\displaystyle\int_{Z}|\bar{C}_{z}|^{2}\nu(dz)<~\infty
such that for any
x,x^{\prime},y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
z\in Z

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}b^{u}(x,\mu)-\partial_{x}b^{u}(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle C\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\eta-1}|x-x^{\prime}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu)-\partial_{x}\sigma^{u\ell}(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle C\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\frac{\eta}{2}-1}|x-x^{\prime}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}|\partial_{x}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,z)-\partial_{x}\gamma^{u}(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq

\displaystyle C\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\eta-2}|x-x^{\prime}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mu}b^{u}(x,\mu,y)-\partial_{\mu}b^{u}(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},y^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle C\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\eta}|x-x^{\prime}|+|y-y^{\prime}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mu}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu,y)-\partial_{\mu}\sigma^{u\ell}(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},y^{\prime})|\leq

\displaystyle C\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\frac{\eta}{2}}|x-x^{\prime}|+|y-y^{\prime}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\},

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mu}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,y,z)-\partial_{\mu}\gamma^{u}(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},y^{\prime},z)|\leq

\displaystyle\bar{C}_{z}\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\frac{\eta}{2}}|x-x^{\prime}|+|y-y^{\prime}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\}.\color[rgb]{0,0,0}

Assumption 2.14
.

There exists a constant
C>0
such that for any
x,y,y^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle|\partial_{\mu}^{2}b^{u}(x,\mu,y,y^{\prime})|+|\partial_{\mu}^{2}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu,y,y^{\prime})|+\int_{Z}|\partial_{\mu}^{2}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,y,y^{\prime},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq C

where
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.

Assumption 2.15
.

There exists an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and a constant
C>0
independent of
n,N
such that

\displaystyle E\big|

\displaystyle b\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)-(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}+E\big|\sigma(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n})-(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle+\int_{Z}E\big|\gamma(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},z)-(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},z\big)\big|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq Cn^{-1-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}}

for any
s\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.

Assumption 2.16
.

There exists an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and a constant
C>0
independent of
n,N
such that

\displaystyle E\big|\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n}\big)-(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n}\big)\big|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},

\displaystyle E\big|\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}\big)-(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}\big)\big|^{2}\leq

\displaystyle Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}E\big|\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},z\big)-(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},z\big)\big|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq

\displaystyle Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}E\Big|\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n},z\big)-(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\gamma^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n},z\big)\Big|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq

\displaystyle Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}}

for any
s\in[0,T]
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
,
\ell,\ell_{1}\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
and
i,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.

Assumption 2.17
.

There exists an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and a constant
C>0
independent of
n,N
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Z}E\big|\sigma(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,z),N,n})-(\widehat{\sigma})^{\displaystyle n}_{tam}(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,z),N,n})\big|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}E\Big|\sigma(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},z\big),\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,z),N,n})

\displaystyle\qquad-(\widehat{\sigma})^{\displaystyle n}_{tam}(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},z\big),\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,z),N,n})\Big|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}\int_{Z}E\big|\gamma\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,\bar{z}),N,n},z\big)-(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,\bar{z}),N,n},z\big)\big|^{2}\nu(d\bar{z})\nu(dz)\leq Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}\int_{Z}E\Big|\gamma\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big),\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,\bar{z}),N,n},z\big)

\displaystyle\qquad-(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big),\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(i,\bar{z}),N,n},z\big)\Big|^{2}\nu(d\bar{z})\nu(dz)\leq Cn^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}}

for any
s\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.

Assumption 2.18
.

There exists an
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
and a constant
C>0
independent of
n,N
such that

\displaystyle\int_{Z}E\Big|\sigma(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(k,z),N,n})-\sigma(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n})

\displaystyle\quad-(\widehat{\sigma})^{\displaystyle n}_{tam}(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(k,z),N,n})+(\widehat{\sigma})^{\displaystyle n}_{tam}(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n})\Big|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq CN^{-2}n^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},

\displaystyle\int_{Z}\int_{Z}E\Big|\gamma\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(k,\bar{z}),N,n},z\big)-\gamma\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},z\big)

\displaystyle\quad-(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{n}(k,\bar{z}),N,n},z)+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle n}(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},z)\Big|^{2}\nu(d\bar{z})\nu(dz)\leq CN^{-2}n^{-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}}

for any
s\in[0,T]
and
i,k\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.

The main result of this article is presented in Theorem
2.1
, which ascertains the strong sense convergence rate of the scheme described in Equation (
4
).
One can find the proof at the end of Section
4
.

Theorem 2.1
.

Let Assumptions
2.1
to
2.3
and
2.5

to
2.18
hold with
\bar{p}\geq\max\{2,\eta/8+1\}(\varepsilon+2)(\eta+1)(\eta+2)/{\varepsilon}\color[rgb]{0,0,0}
,
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
.
Then there is a constant
K
, independent of
n
and
N
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}E|x_{t}^{i,N}-x_{t}^{i,N,n}|^{2}\leq Kn^{-1-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}}.

By combining the results of Proposition
2.2
and Theorem
2.1
, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 2.1
.

Let Assumptions
2.1
to
2.18
be satisfied with
\bar{p}\geq(\eta+1)\max\{4(\varepsilon+2)/\varepsilon,(\eta+2)(\varepsilon+2)/\varepsilon,4(\eta+1)\}
where
\varepsilon\in(0,1)
. Then, there exists a
K>0
, independent of
n
and
N
, such that

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}E|x_{t}^{i}-x_{t}^{i,N,n}|^{2}\leq K\begin{cases}N^{-\frac{1}{2}}+n^{-1-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},&\mbox{ if }d<4,\\
N^{-\frac{1}{2}}\ \log_{2}(N)+n^{-1-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}}\ ,&\mbox{ if }d=4,\\
N^{-\frac{2}{d}}+n^{-1-\frac{2}{\varepsilon+2}},&\mbox{ if }d>4.\end{cases}

2.3.
Relevant pre-requisites

For the derivation of the Milstein-type scheme of the interacting particle system driven by Brownian motion and Poisson random measure in (
3
), one requires Itô’s formula as given in Lemma
2.1
below.
Itô’s formula for continuous McKean–Vlasov SDEs is studied in
[
9
]
, which is extended for Lévy noise in
[
26
,
37
]
.
For McKean–Vlasov SDEs with jumps in multiple dimensions, Itô’s formula is investigated in
[
45
]
.
In
[
25
]
, the authors provide an Itô formula for the flow of probability measures of general semi-martingales, which is the most general result available in the literature to date.
In Appendix
A
, for completeness, we provide a different and simpler proof of Itô’s formula (see Lemma
2.1
below) specifically for (
3
) using the empirical projection method, which is of independent interest.

Lemma 2.1

(
Itô’s formula for the interacting particle system (
3
)).

For any real-valued
F\in\mathcal{C}^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}))

\displaystyle F(

\displaystyle x_{t}^{i,N},\,\mu_{t}^{x,N})=F(x_{0}^{i,N},\mu_{0}^{x,N})+\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{b}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N})ds-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{\gamma(z)}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N})\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{b}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N},x_{s}^{k,N})ds-\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{\gamma(z)}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N},x_{s}^{k,N})\nu(dz)ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{x}^{\sigma^{\ell}}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N})dw_{s}^{i\ell}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell}}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N},x_{s}^{k,N})dw_{s}^{k\ell}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{xx}^{\sigma}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N})ds+\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{x\mu}^{\sigma}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N})ds

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{y\mu}^{\sigma}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N},x_{s}^{k,N})ds+\frac{1}{2N^{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\mathfrak{D}_{\mu\mu}^{\sigma}F(x_{s}^{i,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N},x_{s}^{k,N})ds

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=1}^{N}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\Big\{F\big(x_{s-}^{i,N}+1_{\{k=i\}}\gamma(x_{s}^{k,N},\mu_{s}^{x,N},z),\mu_{s-}^{x+\gamma(k,z),N}\big)-F(x_{s-}^{i,N},\mu_{s-}^{x,N})\Big\}n_{p}^{k}(ds,dz)

almost surely for
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
where
\mu_{s-}^{x+\gamma(k,z),N}=\displaystyle\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{x_{s-}^{j,N}+1_{\{j=k\}}\gamma(x_{s}^{j,N},\,\mu_{s}^{x,N},\,z)}
.

We also require the below mentioned elementary result.

Lemma 2.2
.

For any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
p>4
,

\displaystyle|x|^{p}-|y|^{p}-p|y|^{p-2}y(x-y)\leq p(p-1)|x-y|^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\big|y+\theta(x-y)\big|^{p-2}d\theta.

The proof of the following lemma is obtained by a similar argument as for
[
33
, Corollary 4.2]
.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\to\mathbb{R}
be a function having measure derivative
\partial_{\mu}f:\mathbb{R}^{d}\times\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})\times\mathbb{R}^{d}\to\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Then, for any
z,x^{j},y^{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}

\displaystyle f\Big(z,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{x^{j}}\Big)\hskip-1.13791pt-\hskip-1.13791ptf\Big(z,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{y^{j}}\Big)\hskip-0.85355pt=\hskip-0.85355pt\frac{1}{N}\int_{0}^{1}\sum_{\widehat{j}=1}^{N}\partial_{\mu}f\Big(z,\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{y^{j}+\theta(x^{j}-y^{j})},y^{\widehat{j}}+\theta(x^{\widehat{j}}\hskip-0.56917pt-\hskip-0.56917pty^{\widehat{j}})\Big)(x^{\widehat{j}}\hskip-0.56917pt-\hskip-0.56917pty^{\widehat{j}})d\theta.

The proof of the following lemma is given in
[
40
]
.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
p\geq 2
.
For a real-valued
\mathscr{P}\otimes\mathscr{Z}
-measurable function
g
, there is a constant
K
, depending only on
p
, such that

\displaystyle E\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\Big|\int_{0}^{t}

\displaystyle\int_{Z}g_{s}(z)\tilde{n}_{p}(ds,dz)\Big|^{p}\leq KE\int_{0}^{T}\int_{Z}|g_{t}(z)|^{p}\nu(dz)dt<\infty.

In this section, we list some of observations that can be made from the above mentioned assumptions in the form of the following remarks. These remarks are used later in the proofs of the lemmas and the main result.

Remark 2.2
.

Due to Assumption
2.3
,
\displaystyle\int_{Z}|\gamma(0,\delta_{0},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq K
.

Remark 2.3
.

For any
x,x^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu,\mu^{\prime}\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, Assumptions
2.2
and
2.10
yield

\displaystyle|\sigma(x,\mu)-\sigma(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime})|^{2}+\int_{Z}|\gamma(x,\mu,z)-\gamma(x^{\prime},\mu^{\prime},z)|^{2}\nu(dz)\leq K\{(1+|x|+|x^{\prime}|)^{\eta}|x-x^{\prime}|^{2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\mu^{\prime})\}.

Remark 2.4
.

By Assumption
2.10
and Remark
2.3
, for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})

\displaystyle|b(x,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq K\{(1+|x|)^{\eta+1}+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\},

\displaystyle|\sigma(x,\mu)|^{2}+\int_{Z}|\gamma(x,\mu,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq K\{(1+|x|)^{\eta+2}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}(\mu,\delta_{0})\},

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}b^{u}(x,\mu)|+|\partial_{x}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu)|^{2}+\int_{Z}|\partial_{x}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq K(1+|x|)^{\eta}

where
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
.
Similarly, for any
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\mu\in\mathscr{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
u\in\{1,\ldots,d\}
and
\ell\in\{1,\ldots,m\}
, by Assumption
2.13

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{2}b^{u}(x,\mu)|

\displaystyle\leq K(1+|x|)^{\eta-1},

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}^{2}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu)|^{2}+\int_{Z}|\partial_{x}^{2}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq K(1+|x|)^{\eta-2},

\displaystyle|\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}b^{u}(x,\mu,y)|+|\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu,y)|^{2}+\int_{Z}|\partial_{x}\partial_{\mu}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,y,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq K(1+|x|)^{\eta},

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}\partial_{\mu}b^{u}(x,\mu,y)|+|\partial_{y}\partial_{\mu}\sigma^{u\ell}(x,\mu,y)|+\int_{Z}|\partial_{y}\partial_{\mu}\gamma^{u}(x,\mu,y,z)|^{2}\nu(dz)

\displaystyle\leq K.

We use the following estimate for any
x^{j},y^{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
which can be found in
[
21
, Section 2]

(10)

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{2}^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{x^{j}},\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\delta_{y^{j}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|x^{j}-y^{j}|^{2}.

3.
Moment bound of the Milstein-type scheme

In this section, we establish the moment bound (in
\mathcal{L}^{p}
-sense) of the tamed Milstein scheme (see Lemma
3.3
below).
We first prove the following auxiliary results.

Lemma 3.1
.

If Assumptions
2.7
to
2.9
hold, then for any

, there exists a constant

independent of

and

,

and

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathfrak{q=1}}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{p_{0}}\leq Kn^{\frac{2p_{0}-9}{12}}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\big),

\displaystyle\int_{Z}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{1}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{p_{0}}\nu(d\bar{z})\leq Kn^{-\frac{p_{0}}{4}}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\big),

\displaystyle\int_{Z}\Big(E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{2}^{u})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{p_{0}}\Big)^{p_{1}}\nu(d\bar{z})\leq Kn^{-p_{1}+\frac{1}{2}}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}p_{1}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{p_{0}p_{1}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+\frac{K}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\Big|\sum_{\ell_{1}=1}^{m}\int_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{s}{(\widehat{\mathfrak{D}}_{\mu}^{\sigma^{\ell_{1}}}\sigma^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}\big)}dw_{r}^{k\ell_{1}}\Big|^{p_{0}}

(13)

\displaystyle=:T_{1}+T_{2}

for any

,

Lemma 3.2
.

If Assumptions
2.7
to
2.9
are satisfied, then for any
p_{0}\geq 2
,
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}

\displaystyle E_{\kappa_{n}(t)}|x_{t}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\leq K(n^{-\frac{p_{0}}{3}}+n^{-\frac{3}{4}})\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}+\frac{1}{N}\displaystyle\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{k,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\big)

almost surely where
K>0
is a constant independent of
N
and
n
.

Proof.

First, by using Equation (
4
),
Lemma
2.4
and Hölder’s inequality, one obtains

\displaystyle E_{\kappa_{n}(t)}|x_{t}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\leq Kn^{-{p_{0}}+1}E_{\kappa_{n}(t)}\int_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{t}\big|(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{p_{0}}ds

\displaystyle\quad+Kn^{-\frac{p_{0}}{2}+1}E_{\kappa_{n}(t)}\int_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{t}\Big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\Big|^{p_{0}}ds

\displaystyle\quad+Kn^{-\frac{p_{0}}{2}+1}E_{\kappa_{n}(t)}\int_{\kappa_{n}\hskip-2.84544pt(t)}^{t}\hskip-2.84544pt\int_{Z}\Big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\hskip-1.42271pt+\hskip-1.42271pt\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\Big|^{p_{0}}\nu(d\bar{z})ds

\displaystyle\quad+KE_{\kappa_{n}(t)}\int_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{t}\int_{Z}\Big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\Big|^{p_{0}}\nu(d\bar{z})ds

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Also, the application of Assumption
2.7
and Lemma
3.1
yields

\displaystyle E_{\kappa_{n}(t)}|x_{t}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\leq K\big(n^{-\frac{2p_{0}}{3}}+n^{-\frac{p_{0}}{3}}+n^{-\frac{4p_{0}+9}{12}}+n^{-\frac{3}{4}}+n^{-\frac{p_{0}+4}{4}}+n^{-\frac{3}{2}}\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{k,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\big)\leq K(n^{-\frac{p_{0}}{3}}+n^{-\frac{3}{4}})\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(t)}^{k,N,n}|^{p_{0}}\big)

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
This completes the proof.
∎

As the interacting particle system (
3
) is regarded as a
d\times N
dimensional SDEs, its Milstein-type scheme (
4
) can also be considered as
d\times N
dimensional SDEs.
By Assumption
2.7
, we notice that the coefficients of the Milstein-type scheme (
4
) satisfy linear growth type conditions, although the constants depend on the number of sub-intervals
n
and of particles
N
.
As a consequence of this and the finiteness of the discretisation points, one can immediately notice that

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}E|x_{t}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}<K

where
K>0
depends on
n
and
N
which explode when
n
and
N
tend to infinity.
In the following lemma, we establish that
K
is independent of
n
and
N
and thus the Milstein-type scheme (
4
) has bounded moments.

Lemma 3.3
.

If Assumptions
2.1
and
2.6
to
2.9
are satisfied, then the following holds

\displaystyle\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}E|x_{t}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\leq K

where
K>0
is a constant independent of
N
and
n
.

Proof.

Let us recall the Milstein-type scheme (
4
) and use Itô’s formula (see
[
46
, Theorem 94]
) to obtain

\displaystyle|x_{t}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}=|x_{0}^{i}|^{\bar{p}}+{\bar{p}}\int_{0}^{t}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{s}^{i,N,n}(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)ds

\displaystyle\quad+{\bar{p}}\int_{0}^{t}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{s}^{i,N,n}\Big\{(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\Big\}dw_{s}^{i}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{\bar{p}(\bar{p}-1)}{2}\int_{0}^{t}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}ds

\displaystyle\quad+{\bar{p}}\hskip-2.84544pt\int_{0}^{t}\hskip-2.84544pt\int_{Z}\hskip-0.28436pt|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{s}^{i,N,n}\hskip-1.9919pt\Big\{(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\hskip-1.9919pt+\hskip-1.9919pt\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\Big\}\tilde{n}_{p}^{i}(ds,d\bar{z})

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\Big\{\big|x_{s}^{i,N,n}+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}}-|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle\quad-{\bar{p}}\,|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{s}^{i,N,n}\Big((\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\Big)\Big\}n_{p}^{i}(ds,d\bar{z})

almost surely for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
which on talking expectation and using Assumption
2.6
for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
yield

\displaystyle E|x_{t}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\leq E|x_{0}^{i}|^{\bar{p}}+\frac{\bar{p}}{2}E\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{2|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)

\displaystyle\,\,\,+(\bar{p}-1)|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\,\,\,+2(\bar{p}-1)\int_{Z}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\big|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}+\theta(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}d\theta\nu(d\bar{z})\Big\}ds

\displaystyle+{\bar{p}}E\int_{0}^{t}\big\{|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{s}^{i,N,n}-|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}\big\}(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)ds

\displaystyle+\frac{\bar{p}(\bar{p}-1)}{2}E\int_{0}^{t}\big\{|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}-|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big\}\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}ds

\displaystyle+\frac{\bar{p}(\bar{p}-1)}{2}E\int_{0}^{t}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\Big\{\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)|^{2}\Big\}ds

\displaystyle+E\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\Big\{\big|x_{s}^{i,N,n}+(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}}-|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle\quad-{\bar{p}}\,|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{s}^{i,N,n}\Big((\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\Big)

\displaystyle\quad-\bar{p}(\bar{p}-1)\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-\theta)\big|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}+\theta(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}d\theta\Big\}\nu(d\bar{z})ds

(20)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\leq E|x_{0}^{i}|^{\bar{p}}+KE\int_{0}^{t}\big\{1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{p_{0}}+\mathcal{W}_{2}^{p_{0}}(\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},\delta_{0})\big\}ds+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{4}\Pi_{\mathfrak{q}}.

To estimate
\Pi_{1}
, one can write

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}:

\displaystyle={\bar{p}}E\int_{0}^{t}\big\{|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{s}^{i,N,n}-|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}\big\}(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)ds

\displaystyle={\bar{p}}E\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{\big(|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}-|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big)x_{s}^{i,N,n}+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big(x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}\big)\Big\}(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)ds

\displaystyle\leq KE\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{\big(|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}+|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}\big)\big|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}\big|\big(|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|\Big\}\big|(\widehat{b})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|ds

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
where the last inequality is obtained by using

\displaystyle|y_{1}|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle-|y_{2}|^{\bar{p}-2}=(\bar{p}-2)\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}|y_{2}+\theta(y_{1}-y_{2})|^{\bar{p}-4}(y_{2}+\theta(y_{1}-y_{2}))(y_{1}-y_{2})d\theta

(21)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\bar{p}-2)\int_{0}^{1}|y_{2}+\theta(y_{1}-y_{2})|^{\bar{p}-3}|y_{1}-y_{2}|d\theta\leq K\big(|y_{2}|^{\bar{p}-3}+|y_{1}-y_{2}|^{\bar{p}-3}\big)|y_{1}-y_{2}|

for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
.
Further, one uses Assumption
2.7
, Young’s inequality and Lemma
3.2
to get

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}

\displaystyle\leq KE\int_{0}^{t}n^{\frac{1}{3}}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},\delta_{0})\big)\Big(|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|

\displaystyle\qquad+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{2}+|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-1}\Big)ds

\displaystyle\leq KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}n^{\frac{1}{3}}\Big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{j,N,n}|\Big)\Big(|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}n^{-\frac{1}{3}}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}n^{\frac{1}{3}}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{2}+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}+E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-1}\Big)ds\Big\}

\displaystyle\leq Kn^{\frac{1}{3}}\big(n^{-\frac{1}{3}}+n^{-\frac{2}{3}}+\epsilon_{n}(\bar{p})+n^{-\frac{3}{4}}\big)E\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\big)ds

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
where the following estimate is also used (due to Lemma
3.2
)

(22)

\displaystyle E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\leq K\epsilon_{n}(\bar{p})\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\big)

for any
\bar{p}>4
with

\epsilon_{n}(\bar{p}):=\begin{cases}n^{-\frac{3}{4}},&\mbox{ if }\,\,17/4\leq\bar{p},\\
n^{-\frac{\bar{p}-2}{3}},&\mbox{ if }\,\,4<\bar{p}<17/4\end{cases}.

Thus, one obtains,

(23)

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}\leq K\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{r\in[0,s]}E|x_{r}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\big)ds

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
For
\Pi_{2}
, one applies Equation (
21
) to get

\displaystyle\Pi_{2}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\bar{p}(\bar{p}-1)}{2}E\int_{0}^{t}\big\{|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}-|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\big\}\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle KE\int_{0}^{t}\big\{|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}+|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}\big\}\big|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}\big|\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle KE\int_{0}^{t}\big\{|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-3}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\big\}\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}ds

which on applying Assumption
2.7
, Young’s inequality, Lemma
3.2
and Equation (
22
) yield

\displaystyle\Pi_{2}\leq

\displaystyle KE\int_{0}^{t}n^{\frac{1}{3}}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},\delta_{0})\big)^{2}\Big(|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-4}n^{-\frac{1}{3}}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{2}

\displaystyle+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-4}n^{\frac{1}{3}}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{2}+E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\Big)ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Kn^{\frac{1}{3}}(n^{-\frac{1}{3}}+\epsilon_{n}(\bar{p}))E\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\big)ds

(24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle K\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+\sup_{i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}}\sup_{r\in[0,s]}E|x_{r}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\big)ds

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
To estimate
\Pi_{3}
, one uses

|y_{1}+y_{2}|^{2}-|y_{1}|^{2}=|y_{2}|^{2}+2\displaystyle\sum_{u=1}^{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}y_{1}^{u\ell}y_{2}^{u\ell}
for any
y_{1},y_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times m}

as follows

\displaystyle\Pi_{3}:=

\displaystyle\frac{\bar{p}(\bar{p}-1)}{2}E\int_{0}^{t}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}}-2}\Big\{\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad-|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)|^{2}\Big\}ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle KE\int_{0}^{t}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}+x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{{\bar{p}-2}}\Big\{\big|\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle\qquad+2\sum_{u=1}^{d}\sum_{\ell=1}^{m}(\widehat{\sigma}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}}^{u\ell})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\Big\}ds

which on using Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and Assumption
2.7
yield

\displaystyle\Pi_{3}\leq

\displaystyle KE\int_{0}^{t}\big(|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}+|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\big)\Big\{\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle+\big|(\widehat{\sigma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|\Big\}ds

\displaystyle\leq KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\Big(\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle+n^{\frac{1}{6}}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},\delta_{0})\big)\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|\Big)ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\int_{0}^{t}\Big\{|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}n^{\frac{3(\bar{p}-2)}{4\bar{p}}}n^{-\frac{3(\bar{p}-2)}{4\bar{p}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}

\displaystyle+n^{\frac{1}{6}}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+\mathcal{W}_{2}(\mu_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{x,N,n},\delta_{0})\big)|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}n^{\frac{7(\bar{p}-2)}{12(\bar{p}-1)}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\times n^{-\frac{7(\bar{p}-2)}{12(\bar{p}-1)}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|\Big\}ds

for any
t\in[0,T]
and
i\in\{1,\ldots,N\}
.
Further, by using Lemma
3.1
, Hölder’s inequality, Young’s inequality and Lemma
3.2
, one obtains

\displaystyle\Pi_{3}\leq Kn^{-\frac{5}{12}}E\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\big)ds+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{j,N,n}|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times n^{\frac{1}{6}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\Big(E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\Big(n^{\frac{3}{4}}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}+n^{-\frac{3(\bar{p}-2)}{8}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}}\Big)ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+Kn^{\frac{1}{6}}E\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{j,N,n}|\big)

\displaystyle\qquad\times E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\Big(n^{\frac{7}{12}}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-1}+n^{-\frac{7(\bar{p}-2)}{12}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\sigma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-1}\Big)ds\Big\}

\displaystyle\quad\leq K\big(1+n^{-\frac{1}{24}}+n^{-\frac{3(\bar{p}-2)}{8}}n^{\frac{2\bar{p}-9}{12}}+n^{\frac{1}{6}}n^{-\frac{7(\bar{p}-2)}{12}}n^{\frac{2\bar{p}-11}{12}}\big)E\int_{0}^{t}\big(1+|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}+\frac{1}{N}\sum_{k=1}^{N}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{k,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}\big)ds

(25)

\displaystyle+\hskip-0.71114pt\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|\big|\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|\Big\}\hskip-0.71114pt\Big\{|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\hskip-0.71114pt+\hskip-0.71114pt|x_{s}^{i,N,n}\hskip-0.71114pt-\hskip-0.71114ptx_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle+\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}+\big|\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}\Big\}\nu(d\bar{z})ds

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle KE\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\Big\{\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{2}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{2}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}}

\displaystyle+\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big||x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle+\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big||x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle+\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-1}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|

\displaystyle+\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-1}\Big\}\nu(d\bar{z})ds

which on using Young’s inequality yields

\displaystyle\Pi_{42}\leq KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{2}\nu(d\bar{z})ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}}\nu(d\bar{z})ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}}ds\Big\}+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-2}

\displaystyle\qquad\times n^{-\frac{\bar{p}-2}{4\bar{p}}}n^{\frac{\bar{p}-2}{4\bar{p}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{2}\nu(d\bar{z})ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}|x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-2}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\times n^{-\frac{1}{8}}n^{\frac{1}{8}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|\nu(d\bar{z})ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|n^{-\frac{1}{4\bar{p}}}n^{\frac{1}{4\bar{p}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\times\Big(\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-1}+E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}|x_{s}^{i,N,n}-x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n}|^{\bar{p}-1}\Big)\nu(d\bar{z})ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-1}

\displaystyle\qquad\times n^{-\frac{\bar{p}-1}{4\bar{p}}}n^{\frac{\bar{p}-1}{4\bar{p}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|\nu(d\bar{z})ds\Big\}

\displaystyle+KE\Big\{\int_{0}^{t}\int_{Z}\big|(\widehat{\gamma})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\times n^{-\frac{1}{4\bar{p}}}n^{\frac{1}{4\bar{p}}}\sum_{\mathfrak{q}=1}^{2}E_{\kappa_{n}(s)}\big|(\widehat{\gamma}_{\mathfrak{q}})_{tam}^{\displaystyle{n}}\big(\kappa_{n}(s),s,x_{\kappa_{n}(s)}^{i,N,n},\mu^{x,N,n}_{\kappa_{n}(s)},\bar{z}\big)\big|^{\bar{p}-1}\nu(d\bar{z})ds\Big\}

for any

and

.
Furthermore, by applying Lemmas
